## Motion Authoring, Editing & Retargeting

- 2026-11-05, **REACT: Towards Real-Time Completion of Skeletal Motion Signals**, P. de Clermont Gallerande et.al., Paper: [@2611.08301](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.08301)
> **Abstract:** Tracking user motion in interactive and immersive applications is increasingly common (e.g., video games, XR systems, smartphones). Yet, processing and transmitting human motion data remain critical challenges, as current approaches strongly rely on the quality of the tracked motion signals. In general, affordable motion-capture systems may produce unnatural animations in the presence of calibration errors, occlusions, sensor drift, high-frequency noise, or other artifacts. More specifically, we simulate occlusions and high-frequency noise and propose to mitigate their effects with REACT, an interactive online motion-completion framework operating causally, frame by frame, without look-ahead. REACT is an S4-based autoregressive model that coordinates complementary motion estimates under specific reliability-dependent constraints and exposes several interpretable internal signals. We present a system-level contribution consisting of a structured formulation of interactive motion completion together with a coherent instantiation of this formulation. We evaluate REACT against several causal baselines and architectural variants under varying levels of occlusion severity, and report their respective inference latencies. Our results show that REACT achieves a relevant compromise between reconstruction accuracy, update smoothness, and interactive inference. Finally, we characterize how its fusion behaviour changes under missing input observations.

## Character Motion Synthesis & Animation

- 2026-11-18, **Probing Physics in Motion Diffusion Models Against Their Own Estimate**, Saeed Ghorbani et.al., Paper: [@2611.24423](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24423)
> **Abstract:** Recent studies report that video diffusion models "know more than they show": physical properties decode better from their activations than from their inputs or outputs. But a diffusion model computes its clean-sample estimate from those same activations, so such a comparison measures how easily a readout extracts the information. Whether the model hides anything is a separate question. We set out to find hidden physical knowledge in kinematic text-to-motion diffusion models, where the physics of every intermediate estimate can be computed exactly, and recorded every layer along the sampling trajectories of MDM (two checkpoints) and MotionDiffuse. Against the usual baselines the layers do look ahead of the model's own estimate on balance and flight. With every readout tuned the same way, the estimate closes the gap: for balance and flight, adding a layer gains at most 0.01, a size that a positive control shows we would detect. The fall rate of jumps is the one clear exception. Early in denoising, MDM's layers add about 0.08 in $R^2$ (up to 0.11 in a refit) that the estimate does not yet show, and the gain fades as sampling proceeds; on MotionDiffuse it is small and not robust. The estimate also flags floating samples without training, but aborting them saves under a tenth of the compute. In a video model, plausibility that linear probes miss in Wan 2.1's clean latent becomes readable after one fixed nonlinearity.
- 2026-11-18, **SemPhys: Semantic-Physical Co-Learning for Humanoid Motion Generation via Differentiable World Models**, Zejian Kang et.al., Paper: [@2611.24074](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24074)
> **Abstract:** In human-robot collaboration, natural language lets people direct a humanoid's whole-body movements without programming joint trajectories. A common approach generates a kinematic reference from an instruction and relies on a tracking controller to execute it. However, as the controller maintains balance under contact constraints, the executed motion can depart from the reference in ways that change the action it conveys. Our central insight is that semantic alignment must be evaluated directly on the complete motion the robot physically executes. Building on this insight, we introduce \textbf{SemPhys}, a framework that trains motion generators using semantic feedback from simulated executions. A differentiable world model and a fixed tracking controller predict a short segment of a recorded execution. SemPhys inserts this prediction at its original position, scores the complete trajectory against the instruction, and backpropagates through the predicted segment. A paired anchoring loss stabilizes adaptation by keeping each generated reference close to its counterpart from the initial generator. On BONES-SEED, SemPhys largely preserves instruction retrieval accuracy from reference to execution and achieves the highest retrieval accuracy on executed motions among the evaluated methods.
- 2026-11-17, **Wear and Go: Calibration Free Sparse Inertial Motion Capture via Online Sensor Setup Estimation**, Yuanshuo Tan et.al., Paper: [@2611.22894](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22894)
> **Abstract:** Sparse inertial motion capture typically assumes that each sensor's anatomical identity and mounting orientation are known. We present MOSAIC (Motion-based Sensor Assignment and Inertial Calibration), a framework that recovers this setup from ordinary motion without a calibration pose. Our central insight is that body pose changes continuously while the sensor setup persists, allowing movements that are individually ambiguous to provide complementary evidence over time. A setup network processes unordered sensor streams with temporal and cross-sensor attention, then accumulates assignment and rotation evidence into a session-level estimate. The recovered setup canonicalizes measurements for frozen pretrained pose estimators, separating sensor configuration from motion reconstruction. For sensors worn at prescribed body sites, evaluation on real inertial recordings with randomized setups shows near-calibrated pose accuracy for bounded offsets and substantial benefits from accumulation under arbitrary orientations. Transfer across pose networks supports setup recovery as a reusable interface between interchangeable sensors and pretrained pose models.
- 2026-11-17, **ForceBody: Force-Paired Parametric Body Motion with Torque Uncertainty**, Joonwoo Kwon et.al., Paper: [@2611.22769](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22769)
> **Abstract:** Forces and torques drive human motion, central to biomechanics, robotics, and physics-aware animation, yet they are often absent from learning-based motion pipelines for body reconstruction and generation. Some motion datasets provide force annotations, but the paired motion sequences are incompatible with the parametric body models used in modern learning pipelines. In addition, existing datasets typically obtain joint torques through inverse dynamics, which is inherently sensitive to the quality of the input kinematics; however, they do not quantify the reliability of these torque estimates. We close this gap with ForceBody, the first dataset to bring ground truth force supervision into a parametric body representation, shipped with per-sample torque uncertainty. ForceBody pairs the SKEL body model with ground reaction forces and inverse-dynamics joint torques across 10,386 motion trials (9.7M frames, 26.9 hours) from 140 subjects. Each torque label is paired with a per-frame, per-joint uncertainty annotation obtained by Monte Carlo sampling through the inverse dynamics pipeline. We benchmark six architectures (MLP, Conv1D, LSTM, GRU, Mamba2, Transformer) on ground reaction force and joint torque prediction from SKEL motion. As one example use of the released uncertainty, finetuning a Transformer with per-sample uncertainty weighting reduces the MAE of joint torque estimation by 5%. Code and data are at https://github.com/DominoAI-Lab/ForceBody-NeurIPS-ED-2026.
- 2026-11-17, **MAVE: Reconstructing Editable 3D Blockouts from Film Shots**, Yifan Chang et.al., Paper: [@2611.22464](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22464)
> **Abstract:** Animated 3D blockouts support previsualization and provide structural conditions for video generation, but manually reconstructing them from reference videos is costly. This limits both their use by creators and the production of paired training data. We introduce MAVEBench, a benchmark of 50 film shots and 416 keyframes for evaluating camera motion, spatial layout, and character blocking. We propose MAVE, a video-to-blockout framework combining Metric observation, Agentic modeling, and evidence-based VErification. It first recovers cameras, human motion, and geometric evidence in a shared metric coordinate system. An agent then constructs an editable environment under constraints from these observations, while programmatic checks report inconsistencies to guide refinement. The outputs are editable Blender scenes and frame-aligned blockout videos. On MAVEBench, MAVE improves overall scores by 27.5-41.1 points across three agents over their corresponding vanilla baselines. With DeepSeek-1017, it achieves a score of 73.2 at an average API cost of only USD 0.054 per shot, outperforming the strongest vanilla baseline while reducing total reconstruction time by 53%. We also construct MAVEData-3K, a dataset of 3,000 blockout-video pairs for model training, and will release it publicly. Creators can change viewpoints and character paths, then render the edited scenes to condition video generation. Code is available at https://github.com/yfChang-cv/MAVE.
- 2026-11-16, **DuoFlow: Towards Synchronized Speech and Motion Generation with Fine-Grained Control**, Jun Dong et.al., Paper: [@2611.21530](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21530)
> **Abstract:** An embodied speaker's expressiveness depends not only on what is said, but also on how the voice sounds and how the body moves. However, existing joint speech-motion generation models are largely driven implicitly by transcript semantics or intermediate speech representations, offering little explicit control over motion hierarchy, body parts, or vocal style. To bridge this gap, we construct MotionSpeech, a structured benchmark for controllable joint speech-motion generation that includes transcripts, voice-style descriptions, and motion conditions at two hierarchical levels. Building on this benchmark, we propose DuoFlow, a dual-stream latent flow transformer that jointly denoises speech and motion under a shared noise level and a unified second-based temporal coordinate. Modality-specific residual connectors inject structured conditions into the corresponding streams, layer-wise bidirectional cross-modal attention coordinates their generation, and a monotonic phoneme pathway combines normalized progress anchors with CTC supervision to strengthen speech temporal modeling. DuoFlow provides a unified interface for fine-grained motion and vocal-style control and demonstrates the ability to synthesize high-quality, synchronized, and instruction-consistent speech-motion sequences. Project page, https://jundong-dev.github.io/DuoFlow-project-page.
- 2026-11-15, **SGIP: Learning Scene-Grounded Interaction Priors for Scene-Aware Human Motion Prediction**, Donghyeon Soon et.al., Paper: [@2611.20076](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20076)
> **Abstract:** Human motion prediction (HMP) aims to forecast future movement from an observed motion sequence, and recent methods have incorporated 3D scene geometry and object semantics to improve long-term plausibility. However, the destination of future motion remains ambiguous in cluttered scenes: the same observed motion can lead to different interaction targets or navigation modes. Existing methods either handle this ambiguity implicitly or rely on auxiliary signals like gaze that are hard to obtain in practice. To address this, we propose SGIP, which explicitly predicts where in the scene future motion will be grounded across both objects and open navigation regions, and uses it to condition future trajectory and full-body pose prediction. This scene-grounded interaction prior is learned via intent distillation: at training time only, a teacher model scores candidate scene regions by combining an LLM prompted with a textual description of the person's intent with geometric motion-scene features. A student model learns to reproduce these scores from only the observed motion and 3D scene, making intent and LLM unnecessary at test time. Across multiple benchmark datasets, SGIP outperforms prior scene-aware baselines on both trajectory and pose accuracy in a realistic deployment setting where neither gaze nor intent is available at inference, demonstrating the effectiveness of the proposed framework. The project page is available at https://sgip2026.github.io.
- 2026-11-14, **Spectral Steering: Local Inverse Geometry for Frozen Diffusion Generators**, Angelo Moroncelli et.al., Paper: [@2611.19572](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.19572)
> **Abstract:** A frozen diffusion generator may need to change several output properties while preserving others. We study when guidance faithfully realizes such a structured request and whether local inverse correction improves its direction. Our account links this benefit to the interaction between the request and the objective-specific remaining-sampler Jacobian: conditioning alone is insufficient. Spectral steering combines a request-specific cosine-gain predictor with damped inverse correction and fixed-time relinearization for finite edits. Scalar nulls and matched-movement controls test the predicted mechanism. Across 8,923 instances in a retrospectively identified local regime, predicted and realized gains correlate at Spearman 0.79, versus -0.11 for condition number. Experiments span images, audio, robotics, and control and planning, including complex character motion generation. On 50 paired inpainting cases, four corrections improve unobserved-pixel PSNR over Adam by 3.25 dB at equal total derivative traversals. Together, the analysis and controls connect request-dependent geometry to directional gains and establish where these gains translate into downstream performance.
- 2026-11-14, **ImpedanceMimic: Learned Impedance as Action Space for Force-Accurate Motion Imitation**, Matthias Jammot et.al., Paper: [@2611.19153](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.19153)
> **Abstract:** Recovering the forces behind a motion is ill-posed: different controllers can reproduce the same movement while generating different contact forces. Physics-based motion imitation resolves this ambiguity through simulation, but relies on fixed-gain proportional-derivative (PD) control. We show that fixed gains impose a trade-off between motion and force tracking, as contact and swing place conflicting demands on joint stiffness and damping. We introduce ImpedanceMimic, a reinforcement-learning policy that modulates per-joint stiffness, damping, and position setpoints from motion and force measurements, but requires only motion at inference. Across 129 participants and six activities, ImpedanceMimic outperforms matched fixed-gain PD under every integration scheme, direct-torque, muscle-actuated and existing imitation controllers in kinematic and force fidelity, approaching a dedicated motion-to-force regressor. By expressing gains as multiples of inertia-scaled priors, a single policy controls subject-specific biomechanical models without explicit anthropometric input and transfers zero-shot to unseen humans and a humanoid robot. The learned impedance forms a low-dimensional representation of motor state, shared across bodies, that generalizes to withheld activities: stiffness scales with load, and damping anticipates unloading.
- 2026-11-12, **Ropedia Xperience 2: A Gold Standard for Scaling Physical AI Environments and Behaviors**, Hao Li et.al., Paper: [@2611.17060](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17060)
> **Abstract:** Large foundation models such as GPT-6 Astra and Claude Fable 5.1 have recently been shown to control robots directly from visual observations. They nevertheless remain brittle on long-horizon, contact-rich tasks in real-world environments. Further progress depends on two complementary resources: demonstrations of human behavior that supervise how tasks are performed, and interactive environments in which policies can be rolled out, evaluated, and improved in closed loop. Language models have advanced along well-characterized scaling laws; the corresponding question for physical AI is how capability scales with environments and behaviors, which first requires identifying which data to scale and how to produce and reuse such data continuously. To address this question, we introduce Ropedia Xperience 2, comprising 1,589 released metric-scale 3D scans and a collection of 4,230 egocentric recordings of 74 tasks (131 hours). We further show that each capture can be repurposed for robot learning: scenes are converted into physically interactive simulation environments, human motion is retargeted to whole-body robot trajectories, and additional robot behaviors are obtained through virtual teleoperation and automated long-horizon trajectory generation. We propose this joint capture of environments and behaviors as a gold standard for physical AI data collection, laying a data foundation for establishing scaling laws over environments and behaviors.
- 2026-11-10, **EgoHMP: Achieving Precise Human Motion Prediction in 3D Scenes via Egocentric Cues**, Xin Zhao et.al., Paper: [@2611.13654](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.13654)
> **Abstract:** The complex interactive dependencies between human behavior and 3D scenes make accurately predicting human intent a major challenge for human motion prediction (HMP). While recent studies use human gaze to infer intent, gaze signals suffer from ambiguity, drift, and a reliance on eye-tracking hardware. Research in cognitive science indicates that egocentric views inherently encode potential interactions, while head motion serves as a crucial precursor in motion planning. Motivated by this, we propose EgoHMP, a novel framework that uses egocentric views and head motions as robust carriers of interaction intent. By translating this intent into an envisioning of future interactions, EgoHMP achieves precise HMP in 3D scenes. EgoHMP first employs a vision-motion alignment encoder to align the visual and motion feature spaces. Based on these aligned features, an egocentric intent predictor with a modality-aware modulation mechanism adaptively suppresses invalid interaction cues to predict an accurate interaction probability map. Furthermore, to enhance the stability and realism of the generated motions, we propose a contact-aware motion decoder. Specifically, inspired by the human cognitive process of planning trajectories prior to action execution, we first predict a global trajectory to serve as a self-prompt, effectively mitigating the optimization conflict in diffusion models. Subsequently, we introduce a multi-stage denoiser, which relieves motion artifacts by incorporating human contact modeling through a multi-stage optimization strategy. Finally, a GCN-based motion decoder is employed to synthesize physically plausible and semantically consistent motions. Extensive experiments demonstrate that EgoHMP achieves state-of-the-art performance.
- 2026-11-10, **FlashMotion: Ultra-Fast Long-Term 3D Motion Generation**, Kaixing Yang et.al., Paper: [@2611.13626](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.13626)
> **Abstract:** 3D human motion generation plays a crucial role in game development, extended reality (XR), digital humans, and embodied simulation. Beyond motion generation, practical pipelines include rigging, retargeting, and rendering, making ultra-fast long-term 3D motion generation without compromising quality valuable for real-time interactive applications. Despite promising progress, high-quality long-horizon motion generation under tight inference budgets remains challenging, owing to 2 coupled bottlenecks: (1) generative strategies that require many sampling steps for high-quality 3D motion generation and (2) model architectures with substantial per-step computational and memory costs for long sequences. We present FlashMotion, a co-designed framework for high-quality long-horizon 3D motion generation under a strict inference budget, covering text-to-motion, music-to-dance, and speech-to-gesture tasks. To enhance trajectory-level efficiency, we introduce a 3D motion-space MeanFlow formulation with kinematic and dynamic consistency constraints for few-step generation, together with an Adaptive Soft masking mechanism for temporally coherent motion editing. To improve per-step efficiency, we design a lightweight non-autoregressive BiMamba architecture with condition-guided state selection and AdaIN-based condition fusion mechanism, enhancing cross-modal alignment and generalization to longer motion sequences. Extensive experiments demonstrate that FlashMotion achieves overall state-of-the-art performance in both quality and efficiency across text-to-motion, speech-to-gesture, and music-to-dance generation. The project page is available at https://sun-happy-ykx.github.io/FlashMotion-Project/
- 2026-11-09, **LHNav: Long-Horizon Humanoid Navigation via Multirate Coordination and Physics-Grounded Action Data**, Xueyang Wang et.al., Paper: [@2611.12136](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.12136)
> **Abstract:** We study humanoid navigation across multiple destinations under long-horizon language instructions. This task is challenging because robots must maintain progress across successive goals while continuously producing natural, stable whole-body motion through confined spaces. However, task decomposition in existing methods does not by itself ensure progress throughout execution, while waypoint-based navigation does not guarantee natural, route-faithful whole-body motion. Our key idea is to organize navigation into three complementary roles operating at different time scales: a Brain for task reasoning, a Cerebellum for local motion generation, and a Brainstem for whole-body execution. We realize this idea in LHNav, an asynchronous system that uses progress and arrival feedback to coordinate navigation goals, VLA motion-token prediction, and whole-body control. To train the Cerebellum for natural, route-faithful motion, we develop a physics-grounded data engine that improves physical tracking of motion references along collision-aware routes and pairs the resulting demonstrations with rendered visual observations. Together, these designs allow task planning to proceed alongside continuous movement and extend single-goal navigation to successive destinations with natural, route-faithful whole-body motion. In simulation, our navigation VLA outperforms OmniNav and DualVLN in single-goal success and motion smoothness, with fewer collisions and falls. Using a VLA trained only on single-goal demonstrations, LHNav achieves a 75.56% success rate on long-horizon navigation tasks. Deployment on the Unitree G1 further demonstrates single-goal navigation and zero-shot long-horizon task execution in real-world environments, including layouts with repositioned objects. Project website: https://robbyant-research.github.io/LHNav/.
- 2026-11-08, **Kimodo-Policy: Learning Kinematic Action Models for Humanoid Loco-Manipulation**, Yunheng Wang et.al., Paper: [@2611.11554](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11554)
> **Abstract:** Humanoid loco-manipulation requires continuous coordination of high-dimensional whole-body motion to realize task objectives. While vision-language-action models and world action models transfer semantic and visual-dynamics priors to robot control, these priors do not necessarily capture the structure of robot motion. We introduce Kinematic Action Models (KAMs), a paradigm that treats whole-body motion as a direct pretraining target and uses a pretrained motion generator as the primary action-generation backbone. KAMs ground learned temporal and cross-body coordination priors through task conditioning, visual observations, and execution feedback. We present Kimodo-Policy as an initial instantiation, combining a frozen Kimodo text-to-motion backbone with lightweight ControlNet-based visual conditioning. A root-body-hand hierarchy coordinates locomotion and manipulation, while motion-history conditioning and repeated action-chunk prediction bridge motion synthesis and closed-loop control. Following large-scale loco-manipulation pretraining, the policy achieves the highest overall success among compared policies on multi-task HumanoidArena (72.14%), competitive performance on SIMPLE, and 60% mean success across eight real-world tasks, with 678M parameters (92M trainable). Ablations support the contribution of the pretrained motion backbone. Together, these results provide initial evidence for motion generation as a complementary foundation for humanoid action learning.
- 2026-11-08, **InfCoiL: Coordinated Planner-Controller Learning for Closed-Loop Physics-Based Human-Object Interaction**, Yude Zou et.al., Paper: [@2611.11517](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11517)
> **Abstract:** Text- and goal-conditioned human-object interaction (HOI) provides an intuitive interface for specifying high-level intents in physics-based character animation. Mapping such intents to physically plausible, contact-rich behaviors naturally calls for a generative planner and a physics-based controller, yet existing planner-controller systems either execute plans open-loop or close the loop while keeping the planner fixed, so execution feedback never reshapes what is planned. We present InfCoiL, a closed-loop framework for physics-based HOI from coordinated planner-controller learning. InfCoiL couples a morphology-aware flow-matching planner with a unified multi-morphology controller through a shared morphology interface in an autoregressive plan-and-execute loop, and alternately fine-tunes both components with reinforcement learning from the same physical rollouts. To optimize the flow-matching planner online, we introduce Semantic Flow Policy Optimization (SFPO), which stochasticizes selected denoising transitions with channel-wise semantic covariance over heterogeneous HOI features and assigns group-relative, sequence-level rewards from complete closed-loop executions. On held-out OMOMO sequences, InfCoiL outperforms open-loop hierarchical baselines in goal-reaching task success and physical tracking accuracy. Project page: https://infcoil.github.io/
- 2026-11-08, **Does Pose Encoding Matter? Analyzing Geometric Representations for Discrete Motion Vocabulary Learning**, Amir Kimiyaie et.al., Paper: [@2611.11093](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11093)
> **Abstract:** Compressing continuous human motion into a finite vocabulary of discrete tokens is now the first stage of many motion understanding and generation pipelines. Vector quantization partitions a latent space into Voronoi cells around a finite codebook, but the geometry of that latent space is inherited from the input pose encoding, and it is unclear which encoding admits an efficient discretization. We study four encodings that place skeletal motion in different geometric spaces: Gaussian joint heatmaps on the pixel lattice, which we show to be a morphological dilation of the joint point set by a quadratic structuring function; root-relative joint positions in Euclidean space; local joint rotations on $\SO$ through a continuous 6D embedding; and a redundant full kinematic encoding. For each, we train VQ-VAE tokenizers whose codebooks lie on the unit hypersphere, varying codebook size, temporal stride, and code dimension. Beyond reconstruction, we measure codebook utilization and the effective vocabulary size, i.e., the number of equiprobable spherical Voronoi cells with the same entropy as the observed token distribution. Rotation and position encodings keep converting additional codewords into effective vocabulary, whereas the full kinematic encoding saturates at a few hundred effective cells and heatmaps at fewer than sixty. The smallest code dimension gives the highest utilization for three of the four encodings, and the effect of temporal stride depends on the encoding. Encodings of the same pose family can thus yield effective vocabularies differing by an order of magnitude, a difference that reconstruction loss alone does not reveal, which points to input geometry as a first-order design choice in motion tokenization.
- 2026-11-07, **MotionGraphicsBench: Evaluating Motion Graphics Generation with Programmatic Verification**, Jiaju Ma et.al., Paper: [@2611.10592](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10592)
> **Abstract:** Large language models can generate motion graphics from static images and natural language requests, but it remains difficult to evaluate how well models follow requests that combine spatial, appearance, and temporal requirements. We introduce MotionGraphicsBench, an expert-curated benchmark of 100 instances for animating an input static SVG with a motion edit prompt. By supplying all required visual elements, our editing task reflects a designer-like workflow and isolates motion generation from graphic generation. Derived from real-world Lottie motion designs, our benchmark contains diverse combinations of spatio-temporal animation behaviors. Each benchmark instance pairs its model input with a deterministic, human-readable verification program that accepts different valid implementations and reports which prompt requirements pass or fail, rather than comparing against a reference animation or using a VLM judge. We construct these programs with a multi-agent pipeline using human-labeled animations and synthesized test cases. On a balanced test set of 180 animations, our programs achieve 93.3% raw agreement with human judgments, outperforming frontier VLM judges by 4.4%-12.8% while achieving at least 90% recall for both human approvals and rejections. Across 47 configurations from 32 LLMs and 12 providers, pass@1 ranges from 5.0% to 68.0%. Across 15 models at their highest reasoning effort, additional attempts improve every model, but pass@32 still ranges from 38.0% to 95.0%. Our analysis of verification programs reveals that generated animations often fail to match reference poses, follow requested motion trajectories, or coordinate related events. We fine-tune Qwen3.8-27B with group relative policy optimization (GRPO) using our verification programs as rewards, improving pass@1 from 30.31% to 52.19% on held-out instances. Our project page is at https://motion-graphics-bench.github.io/.
- 2026-11-06, **Improving the Diffusability of Motion Tokenizer**, Guanhe Huang et.al., Paper: [@2611.10160](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10160)
> **Abstract:** Latent diffusion models paired with a variational auto-encoder (VAE) tokenizer have shown promising performance for efficient text-driven human motion generation. However, motion latent diffusion suffers from a generation-reconstruction trade-off: simply increasing tokenizer capacity enhances reconstruction but fails to proportionally improve text-conditioned generation quality. We assume this trade-off stems from a spectral mismatch inside the VAE tokenizer: the encoder yields a latent space biased toward high frequencies, and the decoder introduces temporal artifacts. In this paper, we propose the Diffusable Motion Tokenizer (DiMoT) to improve the diffusability of the tokenizer, rather than scaling up the diffusion backbone. To improve text-conditioned optimization, Critic Spectral Adaptation (CSA) leverages a pretrained diffusion model as the critic to suppress high-frequency latent energy. To preserve motion dynamics, the Alpha-Flow Motion Decoder (AMD) performs generative decoding via average-velocity prediction, enabling efficient one-step decoding. Extensive experiments demonstrate that DiMoT achieves state-of-the-art performance across various challenging text-to-motion benchmarks, yielding superior motion fidelity and text-motion alignment. Project page: https://githubhgh.github.io/dimot/.
- 2026-11-05, **EgoGeneralist: Unifying Egocentric Action-Video Tasks through Video-Native Modeling**, Yanlin Jin et.al., Paper: [@2611.08477](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.08477)
> **Abstract:** Modern video models possess strong visual priors, yet do not natively represent human action in metric 3D space. We present EgoGeneralist, which adapts pretrained video generators to unify action-conditioned video generation, pose estimation, and joint action-video generation for egocentric manipulation. Our role-adaptive action renderer provides video-native representations for multidirectional action-video learning, making metric motion usable as both a spatial control signal and a recoverable generation target. Cross-stream action-video coupling establishes close correspondence between metric motion and visual dynamics through lightweight adaptation while preserving the pretrained visual prior. Together, these designs establish a generalist framework spanning six action-video tasks. With only narrow-domain data, EgoGeneralist generalizes to diverse unseen scenes and interactions with effective action control and visual quality, bridging pretrained visual knowledge and metric 3D human motion.
- 2026-11-05, **REACT: Towards Real-Time Completion of Skeletal Motion Signals**, P. de Clermont Gallerande et.al., Paper: [@2611.08301](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.08301)
> **Abstract:** Tracking user motion in interactive and immersive applications is increasingly common (e.g., video games, XR systems, smartphones). Yet, processing and transmitting human motion data remain critical challenges, as current approaches strongly rely on the quality of the tracked motion signals. In general, affordable motion-capture systems may produce unnatural animations in the presence of calibration errors, occlusions, sensor drift, high-frequency noise, or other artifacts. More specifically, we simulate occlusions and high-frequency noise and propose to mitigate their effects with REACT, an interactive online motion-completion framework operating causally, frame by frame, without look-ahead. REACT is an S4-based autoregressive model that coordinates complementary motion estimates under specific reliability-dependent constraints and exposes several interpretable internal signals. We present a system-level contribution consisting of a structured formulation of interactive motion completion together with a coherent instantiation of this formulation. We evaluate REACT against several causal baselines and architectural variants under varying levels of occlusion severity, and report their respective inference latencies. Our results show that REACT achieves a relevant compromise between reconstruction accuracy, update smoothness, and interactive inference. Finally, we characterize how its fusion behaviour changes under missing input observations.
- 2026-11-05, **IMUDINO: Self-distilled Foundation Model for Inertial Sensing**, Zhoutong Ye et.al., Paper: [@2611.07437](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.07437)
> **Abstract:** We present IMUDINO, the first foundation model (FM) for inertial sensing to achieve competitive performance across diverse sensing tasks including human activity recognition (HAR), health, motion capture, and odometry. Prior inertial FMs often focus on one subfield of inertial sensing and sometimes underperform state-of-the-art supervised methods (e.g., in HAR), making their scope considerably narrower than FMs for vision and natural language. Moreover, they often require fixed input length and sampling rate, and generalize poorly to unseen sensor locations. To build a general-purpose foundation model for inertial sensing, we adapt the DINO self-supervised learning (SSL) paradigm to inertial data through a set of semantics-preserving motion transformations that simulate variations in motion pattern and sensor setup. For model architecture, we use a plain transformer encoder with frequency-aware positional encoding that natively supports varying input length and sampling rate. This simple setup proved remarkably effective for learning global and dense features generalizable across users, sensor setups, tasks, and, in the case of robot odometry, embodiments. We hope IMUDINO can serve as a backbone for inertial sensing and kickstart the development of general-purpose inertial FMs.

## Vision-Language & Action Models (VLA/VLM)

- 2026-11-18, **UltraTex: Aligning Physical Knowledge for High-Fidelity PBR Texture Generation**, Jingzhi Bao et.al., Paper: [@2611.24941](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24941)
> **Abstract:** Generating physically based rendering (PBR) texture maps for a 3D mesh from a single reference image is highly ambiguous, as the observed appearance entangles material identity, surface reflectance, and illumination. While current generative methods produce realistic albedo maps, they still struggle to infer accurate metallic and roughness attributes for relighting. We present UltraTex, a cascaded multi-view framework that moves beyond appearance matching by integrating physical knowledge into multi-view diffusion-based PBR generation. UltraTex first jointly predicts albedo and world-space shading normals to generate more detailed albedo maps, and then predicts metallic-roughness (MR) maps conditioned on the generated albedo, reference image, and material semantic priors. To inject material semantics, a lightweight adapter transforms hidden-layer features of a vision-language model (VLM) into intrinsic material representation tokens, providing part-level knowledge anchors for MR estimation. To model material-light interaction, photometric representation alignment guides intermediate diffusion features toward ground-truth material renderings under controlled lighting. This makes metallic and roughness variations observable through highlight intensity, specular sharpness, and local reflectance contrast, providing stronger guidance for fine-grained material prediction without explicit lighting supervision. Extensive experiments show that UltraTex generates high-quality textures and outperforms existing open-source and commercial methods in visual fidelity, material accuracy, and physical plausibility.
- 2026-11-18, **$Z$-sight: Unifying Vision-Language-Action and Latent World Modeling**, Junjie Ye et.al., Paper: [@2611.24940](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24940)
> **Abstract:** We introduce $Z$-sight, a unified robot foundation model that couples vision-language-action (VLA) and generative latent world modeling. Video-based world action models (WAMs) show the value of joint future-and-action generation for control, yet inherit the heavy cost of video generators. Recent latent-space policies avoid pixel generation, yet typically align intermediate features or rely on frozen target encoders, decoupling future prediction from the VLM's native feature space. $Z$-sight instead predicts the future directly in the visual token space in which its VLM perceives the present. Coupling a foresight expert and a continuous action expert with the VLM in a mixture-of-transformers, $Z$-sight jointly denoises future spatial tokens and actions at inference without pixel reconstruction or external encoders. Trained on human video and robot trajectories, $Z$-sight achieves 81.9% success on SIMPLE (+9.6 points over the strongest baseline) and 73.3% across six Unitree G1 tasks (+20.0 points) at 110 ms per chunk, while remaining competitive on tabletop benchmarks.
- 2026-11-18, **Phoenix: Learning Scene Structure Representations for Generalizable World-Action Models**, Yupeng Zheng et.al., Paper: [@2611.24938](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24938)
> **Abstract:** World-action models jointly predict future observations and actions, yet reconstruction-oriented visual representations do not explicitly emphasize the spatial relationships and object-state changes needed for manipulation. We introduce Phoenix, which learns representations of scene structure for world-action modeling. Specifically, Phoenix Encoder enriches DINOv3's general visual semantics through dual-teacher distillation of geometric and spatiotemporal semantic knowledge. Next, CS-VAE causally aggregates and compresses Phoenix Encoder features into generative latents while preserving scene information and spatial layout. Finally, Phoenix jointly predicts these latents, complementary appearance latents, and robot actions, linking scene evolution with action generation. To study pretraining data efficiency, we curate approximately 39,000 hours of egocentric and robot manipulation data for representation learning and use the robot demonstration subset for WAM pretraining. Following benchmark-specific post-training, Phoenix achieves state-of-the-art performance across simulation and real-robot benchmarks spanning single-arm, bimanual, and dexterous manipulation. It demonstrates strong robustness to perturbations, achieving success rates of 94.8% on LIBERO-Plus and 72.4% on RoboTwin 2.0 C2R, alongside strong cross-embodiment transfer and real-world manipulation performance on Bench2Dex and RoboDojo-RealWorld, respectively. Pretraining-scale analysis shows larger performance gains from data scaling than a video-VAE-only baseline, indicating stronger scaling benefits and more effective data utilization. Moreover, Phoenix maintains high success rates when adapted with limited downstream demonstrations, demonstrating strong few-shot adaptation. Our code, pretraining data, and models are available on the project page to help the community reproduce our results.
- 2026-11-18, **Grana: Compositional Reward Modeling for Long-Horizon Robot Manipulation**, Zi-ang Cao et.al., Paper: [@2611.24926](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24926)
> **Abstract:** Robot reward modeling faces two challenges: generalizing across tasks and accurately tracking progress in long-horizon manipulation. General-purpose reward models demonstrate broad task transfer, but transfer from short-task pretraining to long-horizon tasks remains underexplored. Long-horizon progress models often rely on task-specific subtask classifiers or fixed action vocabularies, limiting the reuse of learned progress knowledge when adapting to new tasks. To address these challenges, we introduce Grana, a vision-language robot reward model that learns to identify unfinished subtasks and estimate their continuous progress using open-vocabulary subtask descriptions. We train a language-conditioned model to estimate progress in short-horizon tasks and individual subtasks of long-horizon tasks. For long-horizon tasks, we combine the selected subtask's predicted progress with credit for preceding completed subtasks to estimate whole-task progress. General progress prediction requires training the model across diverse tasks with progress supervision that accounts for imperfect execution. Elapsed-time proxies can misrepresent progress when relative subtask durations vary, or failures reverse local progress. We therefore introduce an annotation protocol that converts sparse subtask boundaries and action-quality annotations into dense progress targets, without requiring annotators to assign numerical progress scores. Pretraining with this supervision on 200 diverse, mostly short-horizon manipulation tasks enables zero-shot reward prediction on unseen tasks and improves progress prediction after adaptation to multi-minute, long-horizon tasks. Experiments demonstrate (i) generalizable reward prediction, including adaptation to five long-horizon tasks, (ii) benefits from pretraining and composing subtask progress estimates, and (iii) improved downstream policy performance.
- 2026-11-18, **NereidVLA: Learning to Understand and Navigate the Underwater World**, Chenjie Zhao et.al., Paper: [@2611.24864](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24864)
> **Abstract:** Underwater navigation requires translating visual spatial cues into motion, yet marine vision-language datasets rarely include paired robot trajectories. We present NereidVLA, a vision-language-action model that transfers marine spatial knowledge to language-conditioned 3D navigation. We first train Sea2Know on approximately two million marine examples to learn target grounding, spatial relations, and scene structure without paired robot-motion supervision. We then adapt it using expert trajectories executed under simulated underwater vehicle dynamics. Given RGB histories and a language instruction, NereidVLA predicts discrete motion tokens that decode into local 3D trajectories for closed-loop tracking. Evaluation combines an independent six-dimensional underwater navigation understanding benchmark with offline prediction and closed-loop navigation. Under matched navigation training and execution settings, marine spatial pretraining increases simulation success from 72.4% to 84.4%. Without fine-tuning on the real test environment, NereidVLA achieves 78.75% success in laboratory-pool robot trials, compared with 47.50% for InternVLA-N1. These results show that marine spatial supervision can support downstream motion learning and physical underwater navigation.
- 2026-11-18, **DiWALI: Diffusion Policy Learning in World Models via Latent Action Inference from Human and Robot Play**, Akshay L Chandra et.al., Paper: [@2611.24836](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24836)
> **Abstract:** Learning robot manipulation is constrained by the availability of action-labeled robot data, for both imitation and world models. Humans can demonstrate skills in seconds, but their demonstrations lack the required robot actions. We introduce DiWALI, a framework that learns and improves diffusion policies within a world model trained on human and robot play. DiWALI infers latent actions from consecutive frames using an inverse dynamics model, enabling world model training without action labels, and grounds them in robot commands through a decoder trained separately on robot play. Skill policies are trained over latent actions from action-free human demonstrations and improved with reinforcement learning in imagination. On the CALVIN benchmark, DiWALI policies trained solely from human demonstrations substantially outperform baselines trained with robot demonstrations. Incorporating human play into the world model training also yields stronger policies after fine-tuning than using the same amount of robot play alone. Experiments on a physical robot show that skills learned primarily from human demonstrations are further improved in imagination with a world model trained on human and robot play. Together, these results demonstrate that human experience can support both manipulation skill acquisition and subsequent policy improvement, reducing reliance on action-labeled robot data. Videos, dataset and code are available at https://diwali.cs.uni-freiburg.de.
- 2026-11-18, **RoboMorpher: Scaling Robot Morphology Representations for Cross-Embodiment Action Prediction**, Wenwei Lin et.al., Paper: [@2611.24816](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24816)
> **Abstract:** Learning shared action representations is central to cross-embodiment manipulation. However, identical joint values can produce different spatial configurations across robots, limiting representations learned from joint values alone. We introduce RoboMorpher, a morphology-conditioned action tokenizer that jointly reconstructs joint angles and link poses to associate control coordinates with their kinematic effects. MorphGen supplies scalable kinematic supervision by generating serial-arm descriptions from survey-derived geometric priors. We pretrain RoboMorpher on 40 million morphology-state pairs and integrate the frozen tokenizer into a vision-language-action policy for discrete prediction and continuous refinement. We also introduce EmbodiBench, a ten-task benchmark with 30 generated URDFs per task. With downstream URDFs excluded from tokenizer pretraining, the policy achieves 56.40% success on RoboTwin 2.0 and 23.27% on EmbodiBench, exceeding continuous joint-space control by 9.73 and 15.34 percentage points. - 2026-11-18, **Read Together, Answer Apart: How Earlier Questions Help Vision-Language Models Look**, Xinyi Guo et.al., Paper: [@2611.24785](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24785)
> **Abstract:** Vision-language models (VLMs) are often asked several questions about the same image in a single prompt (co-asking), yet existing evaluations report conflicting accuracy gains and hallucinations at the prompt level without tracking how individual answers change. By pairing each question's single-question and co-asked answers across 22 open-weight VLMs (1B-35B), we find that erratic net shifts reflect two opposing effects: on Densely Captioned Images (DCI), every model repairs 15.4%-29.8% of its single-question errors under co-asking, while the share of correct answers turned wrong falls from 30.8% on weaker models to 4.7% on stronger ones. Across all 14 models supporting custom attention masks, we show through attention readouts and layer-window interventions that earlier same-image questions read visual information into their question tokens in the early-to-middle layers during prefill, helping later questions concentrate image attention onto their queried targets. This visual help accumulates across the scene even across non-overlapping regions, whereas blocking earlier questions from attending to the image lowers both target localization and accuracy in all 14 models. While cross-question help is established during shared question reading, part of the co-asking damage enters later during answer decoding when the decoder attends to other question stems. We therefore introduce Read Together, Answer Apart, a training-free decoding mask that keeps shared prefill intact and isolates each answer from other stems, improving co-asking accuracy on 12 of 14 models on DCI (up to +4.71 percentage points, closing 70% of the pooled gap to asking alone) and on 10, 8, and 5 of the 14 models on WebUI, FinTabNet, and GQA (up to +11.4, +5.3, and +1.1 points).
- 2026-11-18, **Jeveration: System One Models Now Generate**, Yuhe Liu et.al., Paper: [@2611.24738](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24738)
> **Abstract:** System One models such as Jev return decisions through fast, parallel prediction. In this paper, we introduce Jeveration, showing that the same decision paradigm can also generate high-quality images in a single forward pass. Jeveration builds on a frozen vision-language model, repurposing its visual-semantic knowledge for holistic image generation. The key challenge is to maintain global consistency. A single prompt admits many valid images, yet parallel predictions must converge on a coherent whole. We therefore condition all positions on a shared random state to coordinate parallel predictions, so that each draw yields a different but coherent image. Since the model tends to ignore this state under direct image supervision, we first learn a distribution over discrete image codes to guide single-pass generation. Beyond text-to-image generation, we further unify image-to-image generation and dense prediction within the same architecture, so that one checkpoint supports all three tasks, each in one forward pass. Experiments show that Jeveration generates a 512x512 image in 78 ms, up to 122x faster than existing unified models, while maintaining competitive image quality and dense prediction performance.
- 2026-11-18, **HandWorldBench: A Unified Benchmark for Hand-Centric World Models**, Zhaolu Wang et.al., Paper: [@2611.24731](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24731)
> **Abstract:** Recent advances in world models are opening new possibilities for fine-grained embodied tasks, with hand-object interaction offering a particularly demanding setting. Modeling these interactions involves capturing how hand actions change objects and how the resulting states persist over time. Yet systematic evaluation tailored to these hand-centric capabilities remains underdeveloped. To address this gap, we introduce HandWorldBench, a unified benchmark that systematically evaluates hand-centric world models across human hands, simulated dexterous hands, and real dexterous hands along six dimensions: visual quality, hand fidelity, appearance consistency, instruction following, physical consistency, and temporal coherence. To construct the benchmark, we develop a data curation pipeline that extracts hand-object interaction clips and screens them for quality, yielding 20,500 high-quality egocentric clips from over 600 hours of footage. We then manually select 500 clips covering seven task families for evaluation. Benchmarking eleven models under a unified protocol reveals that stronger hand-pose control does not consistently translate into coherent object-state evolution and persistence or robust cross-embodiment performance, with several specialized hand-control models underperforming general-purpose text/image-to-video generators on dexterous-hand tasks.
- 2026-11-18, **DRIFT: Detachable Reasoning for Runtime-Flexible and Efficient End-to-End Driving**, Emad Aghajanzadeh et.al., Paper: [@2611.24719](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24719)
> **Abstract:** The integration of vision-language models (VLMs) into end-to-end (E2E) autonomous driving has demonstrated strong potential by providing scene description and reasoning capabilities. However, VLMs are computationally expensive, and switching language reasoning on and off at runtime without hurting closed-loop performance has not been demonstrated, which hinders practical adoption. To address these issues, we propose DRIFT, an E2E autonomous driving framework that integrates a lightweight language model (LM) for visual question answering and action commentary. By efficiently projecting intermediate driving representations into the language embedding space, we demonstrate that a compact LM (<150M parameters) can achieve strong scene description and action commentary. Furthermore, we introduce a runtime-flexible planning module that is trained on surrogate reasoning features distilled from the LM and can be conditioned on either the LM or the surrogate reasoning features at inference. This design enables bypassing the LM entirely when required by deployment constraints, without sacrificing driving performance. With only 255M parameters, DRIFT reaches up to a Driving Score (DS) of 97.03 and a Success Rate of 89.39% (mean over three runs) on Bench2Drive by interleaving its LM and surrogate reasoning at a chosen reasoning rate. This is on par with the strongest non-language models while being far more compact than existing VLM-based models. DRIFT further achieves competitive results on the real-world NAVSIM and AlpaSim benchmarks at significantly lower computational cost than existing VLM-based approaches. Project page: https://drift-e2e.github.io/
- 2026-11-18, **AggCLIP: Aggregating CLIP for Zero-Shot Multimodal Anomaly Detection**, Zhi Li et.al., Paper: [@2611.24643](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24643)
> **Abstract:** Zero-Shot Multimodal Anomaly Detection (ZSMAD) aims to identify both appearance and geometric defects in novel categories without relying on target-specific training data, making it especially valuable in real-world scenarios constrained by data scarcity, privacy concerns, or high annotation costs. While recent approaches successfully adapt vision-language models like CLIP to achieve this open-vocabulary generalization, they still face significant challenges: (i) severe geometric information loss during 3D-to-2D point cloud rendering; (ii) spatially noisy 2D feature maps produced by raw CLIP patch embeddings, compounded by shallow late-stage modality fusion; and (iii) severe cross-modal semantic misalignment due to decoupled processing streams. To address these limitations, we propose AggCLIP, a novel framework that adapts frozen CLIP representations for ZSMAD by explicitly incorporating local geometric structure and visual spatial context. Specifically, to accurately localize fine-grained defects, we design a Geometry-Aware Spatial Aggregation (GSA) module that refines back-projected 3D point features via density-adaptive neighborhood weighting, alongside a Context-Aware Spatial Aggregation (CSA) module that smooths multi-scale 2D patch embeddings using a lightweight trainable adapter. Furthermore, to prevent semantic drift between modalities, we introduce a Dual-Stream Prompt Contrastive (DSPC) loss, which aligns the abstract concepts of normality and abnormality across the point cloud and RGB streams. Extensive experiments demonstrate that the proposed AggCLIP framework achieves state-of-the-art performance on the MVTec 3D-AD and Eyecandies benchmarks under both one-vs-rest and cross-dataset zero-shot settings, notably surpassing the strongest competitive methods by up to 2.9% in pixel-level AUPRO.
- 2026-11-18, **EgoJev: Finding Evidence in Egocentric Video by Reading VLM Decisions**, Chunggi Lee et.al., Paper: [@2611.24616](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24616)
> **Abstract:** Questions about long egocentric video often depend on one brief moment, such as the second a hand puts an object down. A vision-language model (VLM) that sees a few evenly spaced frames usually misses it. Training-free methods rank frames with a separate image-text model or ask the VLM to write a time. In a controlled test, the VLM itself locates the moment better when it chooses among labeled frames, and we read this choice from its output distribution. With the same candidates, this choice lands on the evidence more often than image-text scores or a written time span, and it compares all candidates in one decision. Based on this finding, we propose EgoJev, a training-free frame selector for wearable assistants. EgoJev reads where the evidence is from labeled thumbnails and looks again at the windows it points to. It places its few answer frames at the quantiles of the resulting distribution. One frozen VLM selects, answers and returns the evidence time, with no second model. A wearable device can encode its coarse thumbnails while it records. With Qwen3.5-4B and 8 answer frames, EgoJev raises QAEgo4D accuracy from 51.2% with uniform sampling to 62.0%, and it outperforms recent training-free frame selectors on QAEgo4D and MyEgo. - 2026-11-18, **SHAPE: Discrete Supervision and Parallel Continuous Streams for Learning Dexterous Manipulation from Egocentric Human Data**, Yaping Li et.al., Paper: [@2611.24585](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24585)
> **Abstract:** Egocentric human recordings are far cheaper than dexterous robot demonstrations, promising stronger pretraining and direct skill learning without task-specific robot data. Yet humans and robots differ in morphology and kinematics, so human recordings lack the joint positions and actuator commands that supervise robot policies, providing only retargeted end-effector (EEF) actions. - 2026-11-18, **BaseRSI: Recursive Self-Improvement of a Base Vision-Language Model via Training-Free Harness Internalization**, Mingda Zhang et.al., Paper: [@2611.24578](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24578)
> **Abstract:** Recursive self-improvement (RSI) is moving from the harness of prompts and tools around a base model toward the model itself. However, existing RSI still faces key challenges, including harness-bound gains that stay outside the base model, black-box supervision that reads only the model's outputs, and imprecise edits whose site and strength are fixed in advance. To address these challenges, we propose BaseRSI, a new paradigm of Recursive Harness Internalization: the base model improves its harness, then writes what the harness adds into its own weights through training-free edits, round after round on its own responses. To support this paradigm, we introduce ReGap, a mechanism that supervises every transformer block with the high-dimensional activation difference that the harness makes at that block alone, and updates the block's weights along this signal in a low-dimensional subspace. Building on this mechanism, we further propose ReKey, an algorithm that writes this signal by regression into a few key-value neurons of every block, and measures the site and strength of each edit on the model's own responses. Moreover, the base model fills every role of this recursion with forward passes alone, and its edits, written at different sites, compose in one checkpoint without refitting. Experimental results on ten visual reasoning benchmarks show that BaseRSI consistently improves a base vision-language model both in distribution and on held-out benchmarks. - 2026-11-18, **Beyond Appearance: Evidence-Guided Programmatic 3D Scene Reconstruction with Agentic Lighting Reasoning**, Zeting Luo et.al., Paper: [@2611.24565](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24565)
> **Abstract:** Image-to-code methods for 3D scene reconstruction produce executable code that generates editable digital content and supports visual simulation. However, existing methods primarily rely on appearance similarity, which provides limited guidance for distinguishing errors in geometry, materials, and illumination. - 2026-11-18, **RECLAIM: Robot-Vision-Informed Adversarial Patch Defense via Embodied Attention Feedback**, Jiayou Zhu et.al., Paper: [@2611.24494](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24494)
> **Abstract:** Vision-language-action policies control embodied agents through a perception-action loop grounded in physical interaction, where adversarial patches can corrupt action predictions and thereby alter subsequent observations. RECLAIM combines this feedback with robot geometry to refine patch localization and select local treatments, then revalidates them as the agent's embodied actions change the observed scene. - 2026-11-18, **Homeward: No Place Like the Training View**, Mingyang Lyu et.al., Paper: [@2611.24455](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24455)
> **Abstract:** Vision-language-action (VLA) policies implicitly assume that third-person cameras remain fixed at their data-collection poses; once a camera is bumped, remounted, or swapped at deployment, task success degrades precipitously even though the underlying task and robot remain unchanged. This degradation concentrates on the thin, articulated robot arm, which feed-forward depth prediction tears and scatters - yet the arm's exact 3D state is already known from robot kinematics. Homeward exploits this asymmetry: it estimates what is unknown - camera parameters and static scene depth - from the live RGB frame and a single training-view reference, and renders what is known - the moving robot links - directly from the kinematic model. - 2026-11-18, **Retroactive Concept Bottleneck Models for Exemplar-Free Class-Incremental Learning**, Yang Ji et.al., Paper: [@2611.24445](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24445)
> **Abstract:** Exemplar-free class-incremental learning (EFCIL) requires models to learn new classes sequentially without retaining training samples from previous tasks. - 2026-11-18, **NativeSeg: Native Vision Language Segmentation of 3D Computed Tomography via Spatial Visual Tokens and Verified Local Zoom**, Chengqian Ma et.al., Paper: [@2611.24443](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24443)
> **Abstract:** Text prompted 3D computed tomography (CT) segmentation requires both full slice context and fine boundary precision across volumes where over $80\%$ of organ prompted slices lack the queried target. - 2026-11-18, **Let a Small Model Read First: Faster Prefill for Long Interleaved Image-Text Inputs**, Xiaolin Lin et.al., Paper: [@2611.24431](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24431)
> **Abstract:** Vision-language models (VLMs) can answer questions about long interleaved inputs, such as documents of many pages and collections of hundreds of images, but they are slow to start: on inputs of about 100K tokens, the prefill takes most of the wait for the first output token. > **Abstract:** Identifying whom a person looks at is central to understanding multi-party conversations, yet gaze geometry alone becomes ambiguous as more people appear. > **Abstract:** Household robots often need to operate appliances, and smart appliances offer both control panels and device APIs. > **Abstract:** Multi-Teacher On-Policy Distillation (MOPD) enables a single student to absorb the skills of several domain teachers by learning from its own generated responses. > **Abstract:** Vision-language models (VLMs) can correctly interpret physics diagrams yet apply constraints outside the states or conditions in which they hold, causing premise errors to propagate through subsequent calculations. > **Abstract:** Ensembles of diverse Large Vision Language Models have demonstrated performance beyond that of single models across multiple datasets and tasks. - 2026-11-17, **Learning Agent-Centric Vision-Language-Action World Models for Autonomous Driving**, Guoqing Wang et.al., Paper: [@2611.22880](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22880)
> **Abstract:** Recent progress in integrating vision-language-action (VLA) models with world modeling has significantly advanced end-to-end autonomous driving by allowing systems to reason about imagined futures instead of merely reacting to current observations. AgentWorld learns these tokens through three key processes: explicit geometric grounding, reasoning-oriented supervised fine-tuning, and agent-oriented reinforcement learning. Extensive open-loop and closed-loop experiments under diverse driving scenarios show that AgentWorld enhances planning capabilities, fully exploits visual evidence and agent dynamics, and yields interpretable agent-centric world states.

## World Models & Generative Video

- 2026-11-18, **$Z$-sight: Unifying Vision-Language-Action and Latent World Modeling**, Junjie Ye et.al., Paper: [@2611.24940](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24940)
> **Abstract:** We introduce $Z$-sight, a unified robot foundation model that couples vision-language-action (VLA) and generative latent world modeling. Video-based world action models (WAMs) show the value of joint future-and-action generation for control, yet inherit the heavy cost of video generators. Recent latent-space policies avoid pixel generation, yet typically align intermediate features or rely on frozen target encoders, decoupling future prediction from the VLM's native feature space. $Z$-sight instead predicts the future directly in the visual token space in which its VLM perceives the present. Coupling a foresight expert and a continuous action expert with the VLM in a mixture-of-transformers, $Z$-sight jointly denoises future spatial tokens and actions at inference without pixel reconstruction or external encoders. Trained on human video and robot trajectories, $Z$-sight achieves 81.9% success on SIMPLE (+9.6 points over the strongest baseline) and 73.3% across six Unitree G1 tasks (+20.0 points) at 110 ms per chunk, while remaining competitive on tabletop benchmarks.
- 2026-11-18, **Render the Robot, Generate the World: One Video World Model Across Embodiments**, Yash Jangir et.al., Paper: [@2611.24925](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24925)
> **Abstract:** Predicting the consequences of an agent's actions requires modeling both the body's motion and the environment's response. Most video world models attempt to learn both from numerical actions, even though geometry and kinematics already specify the intended motion. We introduce Doppel, an embodiment-agnostic video world model conditioned on camera-aligned renderings of future body configurations and numerical action trajectories. By explicitly providing the intended motion, Doppel can focus on predicting object motion and interaction-driven changes in the environment. Rendered motion also provides a common visual interface across different embodiments and action spaces. - 2026-11-18, **Does the Action Matter? Probing Action-Conditioned Semantic Forecasting as a World Model for Autonomous Driving**, Binay Kumar Singh et.al., Paper: [@2611.24840](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24840)
> **Abstract:** World models are increasingly used in autonomous driving to predict how a scene evolves under the ego vehicle's actions, supporting counterfactual reasoning and model-based planning. However, existing evaluations focus on prediction fidelity and planning performance, and rarely verify that the predictions depend on the conditioning action. On real data, feature-wise conditioning depends 1.6 times more on the action than input-level conditioning at similar accuracy, whereas both depend on it equally in the synthetic environment, where the history does not determine the action. - 2026-11-18, **DiWALI: Diffusion Policy Learning in World Models via Latent Action Inference from Human and Robot Play**, Akshay L Chandra et.al., Paper: [@2611.24836](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24836)
> **Abstract:** Learning robot manipulation is constrained by the availability of action-labeled robot data, for both imitation and world models. - 2026-11-18, **Counterfactual World Models: JEPAs That Reason About "What If"**, Randall Balestriero et.al., Paper: [@2611.24778](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24778)
> **Abstract:** World models operate in a world they cannot fully observe. > **Abstract:** Recent advances in world models are opening new possibilities for fine-grained embodied tasks, with hand-object interaction offering a particularly demanding setting. > **Abstract:** Video world models are used as simulators, so when a first frame leaves several physical outcomes open, repeated generations should produce each outcome as often as it occurs in the data. > **Abstract:** Recent studies report that video diffusion models "know more than they show": physical properties decode better from their activations than from their inputs or outputs. ## Computational Design, Mechanism & Fabrication

> **Abstract:** Differentiable simulation propagates derivatives through a physics simulator, turning animation, system identification, control, and computational design into gradient-based optimization. Engines differ in the physics they model and, less visibly, in which mathematical object they differentiate. We separate two questions the literature conflates. The referent is which object the returned derivative belongs to: the finite computation that executed, the converged equation it approximates, or the continuous dynamics. The realization describes the organization of the backward pass: automatically generated differentiation, an equation-level adjoint solve, or a custom adjoint. The referent defines the target against which exactness is assessed; the implementation determines how accurately that target is approximated. Cost depends on the realization together with how the states needed by the backward pass are retained or recovered. Crossing the axes places every method and engine we classify in a cell, separates claims the literature merges, and shows that the apparent trade-off between exactness and memory follows from how methods have been built rather than from a constraint. A second, orthogonal taxonomy covers contact, where the map is non-smooth. We then ask whether a correct gradient helps. Four mechanisms by which useful gradients are lost are cataloged and measured on one testbed, where the advantage of the exact derivative over the equation-level adjoint depends on where the fitting target comes from as much as on the solver budget, and we review the evidence that zeroth-order methods can outperform simulator gradients in the regimes where those mechanisms act. The model classes, systems techniques and applications are surveyed through this lens, and we close with a reporting checklist, a released diagnostic that computes it, and a research agenda.

## Sketch-based & Creative HCI Tools

- 2026-11-16, **Shaping AI's Beliefs Before It Generates: Human Sense of Agency in Creative AI**, Zi Wang et.al., Paper: [@2611.21405](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21405)
> **Abstract:** AI systems for creative media generation expand human capabilities yet risk diminishing human agency. We propose pre-generation belief controls that can empower creators to inspect, revise, and steer the AI's interpretation before any media is generated, so that the initial anchor is set by the human. We formalize four design principles and instantiate them in the Proactive Co-Creator (PaCC), a system for early-stage conceptual ideation and prototyping that proactively surfaces its assumptions and uncertainty through clarifications, belief graphs, and media attributes, inviting users to shape the AI's beliefs before generating the final outcome. Using PaCC as a design probe in a small-scale, single-arm exploratory user study, we observed that participants reported directionally positive sense of agency and process control relative to their familiar prompt-based AI tools, noting that surfaced assumptions helped them think through details they had overlooked, whereas their feelings about ownership and authorship diverged

> **Abstract:** As generative AI becomes increasingly involved in video creation, creators face challenges in expanding, translating, and negotiating creative intent across stages. - 2026-11-08, **From Sketch to Design: A Cross-scale Workflow for Procedural Generative Urban Design**, Jin Gao et.al., Paper: [@2611.11561](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11561)
> **Abstract:** Urban designers use sketches and symbols to express spatial intentions, while many generative urban design tools rely on numeric parameters that are difficult to relate to this visual process. ## 3D Understanding: Reconstruction & Generation

- 2026-11-18, **Multi-Band Galaxy Novel-View Synthesis with Radiative Gaussian Fields**, Yuzhe Wu et.al., Paper: [@2611.24723](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24723)
> **Abstract:** We propose a novel view synthesis tool for galaxies based on Gaussian Splatting (GS). Unlike RGB data, where GS has achieved success, galaxy observations consist of multi-band signals from a massive number of bodies, contain complex patterns of cross-band interactions, and capture light propagation in highly heterogeneous media. As a result, the underlying physical process poses great challenges in model representation and optimization, which have not been investigated in existing research. To this end, we propose a physically inspired Gaussian Splatting method, GalaxyGS, which is equipped with physically meaningful components that capture both the energy emission mechanism of different astronomical bodies and the light propagation between them. In the absence of directly comparable baseline methods, we adopt a wide range of possible alternative approaches. Comparisons conclusively demonstrate that GalaxyGS is better in accuracy and physical fidelity across datasets and metrics.

> **Abstract:** Dense SLAM systems based on 3D Gaussian splatting build maps that render photorealistically, but their surface geometry is poor, and obtaining a mesh requires extraction or fusion stages. ## Embodied AI & Robot Learning

- 2026-11-18, **Phoenix: Learning Scene Structure Representations for Generalizable World-Action Models**, Yupeng Zheng et.al., Paper: [@2611.24938](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24938)
> **Abstract:** World-action models jointly predict future observations and actions, yet reconstruction-oriented visual representations do not explicitly emphasize the spatial relationships and object-state changes needed for manipulation. We introduce Phoenix, which learns representations of scene structure for world-action modeling. Specifically, Phoenix Encoder enriches DINOv3's general visual semantics through dual-teacher distillation of geometric and spatiotemporal semantic knowledge. Next, CS-VAE causally aggregates and compresses Phoenix Encoder features into generative latents while preserving scene information and spatial layout. Finally, Phoenix jointly predicts these latents, complementary appearance latents, and robot actions, linking scene evolution with action generation. To study pretraining data efficiency, we curate approximately 39,000 hours of egocentric and robot manipulation data for representation learning and use the robot demonstration subset for WAM pretraining. Following benchmark-specific post-training, Phoenix achieves state-of-the-art performance across simulation and real-robot benchmarks spanning single-arm, bimanual, and dexterous manipulation. It demonstrates strong robustness to perturbations, achieving success rates of 94.8% on LIBERO-Plus and 72.4% on RoboTwin 2.0 C2R, alongside strong cross-embodiment transfer and real-world manipulation performance on Bench2Dex and RoboDojo-RealWorld, respectively. Pretraining-scale analysis shows larger performance gains from data scaling than a video-VAE-only baseline, indicating stronger scaling benefits and more effective data utilization. Moreover, Phoenix maintains high success rates when adapted with limited downstream demonstrations, demonstrating strong few-shot adaptation. Our code, pretraining data, and models are available on the project page to help the community reproduce our results.

> **Abstract:** Learning locomotion directly on a humanoid robot is a promising direction to avoid the sim-to-real gap. - 2026-11-05, **Latent RL Priors for Data-Efficient Dexterous Manipulation**, Yixiao Wang et.al., Paper: [@2611.08458](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.08458)
> **Abstract:** Learning dexterous manipulation from limited visual demonstrations is challenging because policies must acquire both perceptual grounding and precise control, while reinforcement learning (RL) in simulation can acquire rich control behaviors from large-scale interaction with privileged information. Project website: https://yixiaowang7.github.io/latent-rl-prior-page/.
- 2026-11-05, **4D Point Splatting for mmWave Radar Micro-Doppler Differentiable Rendering**, Adnan Armouti et.al., Paper: [@2611.08342](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.08342)
> **Abstract:** Millimeter-wave radar resolves the micrometer displacements of a moving body as micro-Doppler, enabling gait, gesture, activity, pose and vital-sign sensing. > **Abstract:** Affordance grounding asks which visible part of an object performs a requested operation, a basic capability for embodied manipulation and human--robot interaction. Most existing methods follow a System Two paradigm: a multimodal language model first writes a chain of thought and then turns its conclusion into a box or points for a segmenter. This incurs considerable inference cost, gives the semantic decision little direct control over the extent of the final region, and makes mistakes hard to correct. (1) An inference framework that confirms candidate regions level by level, from object to part to sub-part, with evidence passed between the levels in both directions so that the levels correct one another. (2) A System One model trained for this framework: existing System One models are built for general-purpose decisions, judge one candidate per request and are ill-suited to the framework's region questions, so ours learns directly from the requests the framework itself produces and judges all candidates of a level at once. Trained only on InstructPart and evaluated with definition-aligned operation words, outperforms chain-of-thought methods in domain and in zero-shot transfer to UMD and IIT-AFF, is better overall than existing System One models run in the same framework, and needs fewer judge requests. - 2026-11-05, **TactileLab: Sim-to-Real Dexterous Robot Manipulation with Efficient Shear-Sensitive Tactile Simulation**, Yijiong Lin et.al., Paper: [@2611.07881](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.07881)
> **Abstract:** Robotic simulators that represent shear within tactile sensing are becoming increasingly important for contact-rich manipulation. > **Abstract:** Real-world environments are messy, with paths often blocked by everyday objects like chairs and toys, requiring robots to perform interactive navigation by manipulating obstacles to clear a path.
